data-option=\"0\">A. She denies removing the flower because the family-history entry was false.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. She accepts questions about her own actions while rejecting demands that patients answer for them.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. She claims the family register is the basis of her hospital authorization.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. He spent the next twenty explaining how difficult it had been.","\"The Civic Lineage Office is notoriously selective,\" he said. \"One must possess a surname, a bank account, and a willingness to appreciate a portrait of the founder.\"","\"I have the surname,\" Mara said.","\"You have become controversial about it. That complicates matters.\"","The office had supplied the false genealogy to several charitable publications. Its director, Francis Hale, was retiring that evening. A reception would mark his contribution to the study of distinguished families. Mara wanted to see the original approval form before it disappeared into a successor's inaccessible archive.","Her attorney had requested the document. The office had promised a reply within a fortnight. A courier schedule on its public noticeboard indicated that retired records would be transferred the next morning. The difference made Mara restless.","\"I am asking for a location,\" the attorney told her. \"Not for you to steal a file while everybody watches.\"","\"A modest objective.\"","\"An objective that will remain modest.\"","Cassian said nothing until she put down the phone. Then he held up a pair of gloves. \"You left these in my car.\"","\"An eloquent contribution to the conversation.\"","\"I thought we might dance before you commit an administrative offense.\"","She looked at him. \"You are becoming an unsuitable influence.\"","\"I have had excellent instruction.\"","The reception occupied a converted picture gallery. Velvet curtains divided the main hall from offices where families could pay to discover how distinguished they had always been. A harpist played beside a marble saint. The saint looked as though he disapproved of both the music and the credit terms.","Mara wore a dark green dress with a narrow back and shoes selected for their ability to remain on her feet. Cassian saw her on the stairs and forgot the sentence he had been speaking. She waited, enjoying the effect with no intention of being merciful.","\"You were saying something about the car?\"","\"I have lost confidence in its importance.\"","She took his arm. \"Try to recover before the driver becomes offended.\"","At the gallery, the director greeted them with the bright alarm of a man meeting a problem he had expected to handle by letter. His wife praised Mara's dress and asked whether the news had been very hurtful. Cassian's jaw tightened. Mara gave the woman a pleasant smile.","\"It saved me the trouble of refusing an inheritance I never applied for.\"","An older guest caught Mara's eye over the director's shoulder. Her name card read LEELA RAO. She had worked as a judicial clerk in India before retiring to join her daughter in Asterfall. Hale had apparently spent months trying to establish a prestigious connection in her family, while missing the achievement of the woman standing in front of him.","\"He asked for a portrait of my grandfather,\" she told Mara. \"I offered my law-school photograph. It was not considered comparable.\"","Mara laughed. Leela's eyes warmed.","\"You mustn't let him make you ashamed of the wrong thing,\" she added. \"A false entry can be put right. An absence of curiosity takes longer.\"","Hale returned with a bottle before Mara could answer. Leela accepted a glass, thanked him, and asked in a clear voice when the office intended to explain its error. Several listeners turned towards them. The director's retirement had become less comfortable by the minute.","Hale tried to move them towards the champagne. Mara moved with him, letting the conversation range from family portraits to the upcoming transfer. He described the office as a guardian of civilization, a place where birthright could be understood with proper scholarly care.","\"And how does a claimant approve an entry?\" she asked.","\"A signature. Supporting material. Comparable records.\"","\"In that order?\"","\"Naturally.\"","She thought of the printed flourish beside her flower. \"Then the signed form should be easy to locate.\"","He took a sip of champagne. \"Not during a private event.\"","\"Your event celebrates the work. I thought a demonstration would be especially entertaining.\"","For a moment he had nowhere to put his smile. Then a guest called his name, and he escaped with remarkable energy. Mara watched him go towards the staff corridor instead of the guest who had called.","\"He is going to move it,\" she murmured.","Cassian followed her gaze. \"Or find it.\"","\"Either way, that corridor has become newsworthy.\"","A woman carrying a pearl-handled fan intercepted them before the music began. \"You must be relieved,\" she said. \"It is so tiring to maintain expectations above one's natural rank.\"","Mara looked at the fan, then at its owner. \"I've found it more tiring to repair things people break while explaining my place to me.\"","The woman colored. Cassian took Mara's hand and raised it to his lips, looking neither apologetic nor remotely inclined to move her elsewhere.","\"You're enjoying this,\" Mara whispered when they were out of earshot.","\"Your ability to complete a sentence before I lose my temper is becoming invaluable.\"","\"I had a less polite version.\"","\"Save it. The evening is young.\"","Her anger softened into something bright. He was not asking her to become easier to display. He looked as though he wanted the room to learn how fortunate it was to contain her.","The next dance began. Cassian drew her onto the floor before the office's watchful clerk could decide to follow her eyes. Mara put one hand on his shoulder, the other in his palm. They turned beneath a ceiling painted with heroic ancestors who probably had not deserved the flattering light.","\"You have a plan,\" he said.","\"I am developing one.\"","\"Should I be worried?\"","\"Only if your rhythm is as bad as your breakfast.\"","He spun her once, neatly enough to make her catch her breath. The green skirt moved around her legs like water. For several seconds she forgot the corridor. Cassian knew it, the infuriating man. His thumb brushed her knuckles, and she saw the smile he reserved for moments when she had stopped trying to win.","\"That was deliberate,\" she said.","\"Very.\"","When the dance ended, he kissed her fingers as though there were nobody watching. There were, in fact, a great many people watching. Mara enjoyed that too.","She left him beside Rowan, who began an animated conversation with Hale's nephew about an overvalued family estate. The nephew had strong opinions and weak information. Rowan supplied enough interest to keep him occupied. Mara walked towards the bathroom, passed its door, and slipped behind a curtain into the service passage.","She had restored paintings in galleries. They all had an untidy other side: ladders, spare lights, half-empty bottles, and doors that mattered more than the grand entrance. This one led to a clerk's workroom. Through the adjoining doorway, she could see Hale taking a red folder from a cabinet.","He handed it to a woman in a pearl necklace. \"Private archive. Tonight.\"","\"The legal request arrived this morning,\" the woman said.","\"A request isn't an order.\"","Mara stepped back before either could look towards her. The woman emerged carrying the folder inside a canvas bag. She went not to the lift but towards the cloakroom stairs.","Mara followed.","At the lower landing, the woman stopped to answer her phone. Mara slipped past a rack of stored coat stands, reached the cloakroom from the opposite side, and joined the short line of guests collecting bags. Her heart beat with a familiar, almost cheerful speed. This was a kind of reasoning her hands understood.","The woman placed the canvas bag on the counter while searching for her ticket. A guest's umbrella fell. Mara caught it, returned it with a smile, and shifted the bag away from a spreading puddle. Its flap opened for two seconds.","She saw the first page of the red folder. Her name. The silver flower. The supposed signature.","She photographed the page with the phone already in her hand, then caught sight of a blue receipt clipped beneath it. The receipt belonged to the furniture saleswoman whose brother had taken the old cropped photograph. A purchase had been charged to a communications account. A job reference ran across the upper corner.","The woman reached for the bag. Mara let go at once.","\"Careful,\" she said. \"The bottom is wet.\"","The woman's thanks was automatic. She had no reason to recognize the heiress she had helped invent when that heiress was holding somebody else's umbrella.","Mara walked back towards the hall, keeping her pace ordinary. At the stairs, a hand closed around her elbow.","Hale's clerk stood beside her. \"This area is for staff.\"","\"Then the bathroom signs need work.\"","\"Your phone, please.\"","She looked down at his hand. \"You have confused your employment with an extraordinary range of powers.\"","His grip tightened. Before she could decide whether to make a scene or use the umbrella again, Cassian appeared at the top of the stairs.","\"Mara,\" he said, with a lover's impatience. \"You promised me the next one.\"","He came down slowly. His gaze moved to the clerk's fingers. The man released her before Cassian reached them.","\"We were discussing the signs,\" Mara said.","\"A fascinating subject. Shall we continue on the dance floor?\"","Cassian offered his arm, and they walked away without hurrying. Only when the music covered them did Mara show him the photograph. The signature looked convincing until she enlarged it. A small printed square cut across the tail of the final letter. She knew that mark. It belonged to an old workshop receipt, where a box for payment had overlapped her name.","Someone had not merely misunderstood her family. They had bought a picture, copied a signature, and assembled a claimant.","Across the room, Hale's retirement speech began. Mara put the phone away and moved closer to Cassian.","\"One more dance,\" she said.","\"Before we go?\"","\"Before he discovers how much of his story has gone with us.\""],"questions":[{"type":"inference","stem":"Why does the small printed square across the signature matter to Mara?","options":["It shows that the flower itself was made by the family-history office.","It resembles the overlapping payment box on an old invoice and suggests the signature was copied.","It identifies the date on which she approved the genealogy.","It proves that every family-history record is false."],"answer":1,"explanation":"放大图中签名尾部的方框，与旧工作收据付款栏压住签名的位置一致，提示签名来自另一份文件；并不证明所有家史资料造假。","evidence":[80],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1654,"html":"<article It shows that the flower itself was made by the family-history office.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. It resembles the overlapping payment box on an old invoice and suggests the signature was copied.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. It identifies the date on which she approved the genealogy.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. It proves that every family-history record is false.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"60\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"62\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v06c03","title":"The Girl in the Wrong Coat","volume":"卷六｜被盗名字的审判","anchor":"chapter-0063","paragraphs":["Mrs. Cress opened the workshop door with a broom in one hand and a newspaper in the other.","\"If you've come to buy the building with your ancestral millions, the rent has increased,\" she said.","Mara kissed her cheek. \"I have come to look for a receipt.\"","\"A distinctly less lucrative visit.\"","The narrowed preservation order still covered the disputed cabinet. The rest of Mara's workshop had returned to ordinary life: dust, unfinished work, and a window that refused to close in wet weather. She had paid the overdue rent from her fellowship grant. Mrs. Cress had complained that sudden solvency was making their relationship less stimulating.","The old receipt books stood in a biscuit tin. Mara found the relevant volume by the coffee stain on its cover. The signature reproduced on the false approval page had come from a repair invoice issued to the furniture dealer. The printed payment box cut across the final letter in exactly the same place.","She photographed the whole page, not just the signature, and asked Mrs. Cress to keep the book safe. The original would go through counsel. She wanted no second allegation that she had improved her own records.","Before she closed the tin, Mara noticed a little clock beneath the desk. Its owner had left it before the preservation order. She wound it, listened, and corrected the loose hand that had been brushing the glass. The clock began to tick. Mrs. Cress watched from the doorway.","\"Five minutes,\" Mara said. \"That repair has been waiting months.\"","\"The owner comes by every Friday. She says you are worth waiting for.\"","Mara wrote a collection note and attached the original fee, resisting an impulse to charge nothing because she felt guilty about the delay. Work deserved payment. Delay deserved an apology. She could manage both without pretending her own time had no value.","Mrs. Cress put the clock on the counter where the afternoon light would catch its face. For a moment, the workshop was simply the place Mara had wanted it to be.","On the way out, her landlady stopped her with a hand on her arm. \"I saw what you said at the hospital. About taking the flower.\"","Mara waited.","\"Don't let them make you confess to their inventions because you're prepared to admit your own foolishness.\"","\"I'll try.\"","\"Try harder. And take a bun. You're thinner than your coat.\"","Outside, a man was leaning beside a dark car. Another stood near the end of the lane. Mara had noticed both before she entered. They had made no attempt to look like people with something useful to do.","The man by the car approached. \"Miss Vale? We're collecting papers for the inquiry.\"","\"Then you'll know my attorney's address.\"","\"It will save everyone trouble if you hand over the folder.\"","She held it closer. \"I have been told that before. The trouble rarely belonged to everyone.\"","He showed a card too quickly for her to read. She stepped back towards the shop window, where Mrs. Cress was still visible. The second man moved away from the end of the lane.","Mara decided the conversation had lasted long enough.","She pushed through the nearest shop, crossed its little yard, and climbed the outside stair she had used for years to reach a frame-maker's store. A shout followed. She kept going. The landing opened onto a covered walkway above the market, its roof patched with sheets of transparent plastic. Rain from the previous night dripped through the seams.","Her shoes were not the ones from the reception. She blessed her own good sense for nearly six steps before one boot slid on a wet board. She caught the rail, swung herself around the corner, and landed against a pile of empty baskets. Behind her, a larger body struck the stair hard enough to shake it.","The paper folder would not survive a fall into the market. She tucked the thin copies inside her coat and left the empty folder beneath a basket. A broom leaned against the wall. Mara moved it across the walkway, low enough to delay somebody running without looking, then went down the next stair into a cloud of steam from the food stalls.","She did not stay to admire the result.","At the corner, a woman in a bright yellow coat was arguing about lemons. Mara recognized her from the Lantern Theatre's costume room. The woman recognized the pursuit, which was more useful.","\"Need a change?\" she asked.","\"Quickly.\"","They passed behind a canvas screen. Mara exchanged her dark outer coat for the yellow one, wrapped a theatrical scarf around her hair, and emerged carrying a basket of vegetables. Her acquaintance stepped out on the other side in Mara's coat and immediately began an energetic complaint about the quality of the produce.","The first man ran past Mara without looking at her face. The second slowed, uncertain. She bent over the basket and inspected a carrot with the absorbed judgment of a shopper being cheated. His gaze moved on.","When both had turned towards the yellow-coated woman's performance, Mara slipped into the theater entrance.","Leda Reed caught her in the narrow passage behind the wardrobe. \"Why are you dressed as our retired queen?\"","\"She has excellent pockets.\"","\"The queen was murdered in that coat.\"","\"A shortcoming in an otherwise practical garment.\"","Leda drew her inside and locked the door. Mara took out the copies, checked they were dry, and called Cassian. He answered before the second ring. She gave him the location and a description of the men. Her voice was steady until she reached the part about the card.","\"I didn't see the name.\"","\"You got away. Stay with Leda. I'm coming.\"","She sat on a trunk, suddenly aware of every place she had struck the stairs. Leda handed her water. From the stage came a chorus rehearsing an exile's return. The tune was the one Reed had sung at Kingfisher, and for a second Mara saw the old man's frightened face instead of the costume racks.","Leda noticed where she was looking. \"I've had something from him. Or someone who wants me to think it's from him.\"","She took an envelope from a locked drawer. It contained a small photograph of a station bench, with a chess knight placed on the seat. The knight had a bird attached to its broken head. On the back, in an uneven hand, were the words: \"Still your move. Ask Amina.\"","\"Amina cannot date the photograph,\" Leda added.","Mara looked up. \"Your advocate?\"","Leda nodded. \"She received a message through her office. A man asked whether a visit could happen without anyone from the foundation there. He gave the chess phrase, then said he wasn't ready to say where he was.\"","\"When?\"","\"Two days ago. She didn't want to tell me until she had checked what she could. I asked her not to wait for certainty that might never come.\"","There was a fierce composure in her face. She had spent too long being told what she ought to know about her own father. Mara understood why she wanted control of the next question, even if the answer frightened her.","\"What do you want us to do?\" Mara asked.","\"Let Amina arrange it. No headlines. No arriving with six people and a camera. If he calls again, I want him to hear my voice before he hears another plan.\"","Mara promised. The promise was small enough to keep.","Cassian arrived through the side door with June. His gaze went first to the bruise darkening above Mara's wrist. She stood before he could ask whether she was hurt and kissed him once, hard enough to interrupt the first sentence.","\"I am here,\" she said.","His arms closed around her. For a moment she let herself feel the tremor he was trying to hide. Then he drew back and looked at the ridiculous yellow coat.","\"You look magnificent.\"","\"I have been informed that it belongs to a murder victim.\"","\"We shall improve its record.\"","June was already asking Leda where the exterior cameras pointed. The theater manager agreed to preserve the relevant footage. Mrs. Cress called to say the men had returned to the workshop, demanded information, and discovered that a landlady with a broom could be a difficult governmental institution to negotiate with.","No one had produced a readable credential. Cassian arranged a formal report and asked the attorney whether any authorized collector had been sent. The answer was no.","At the theater's outer gate, an activist was distributing coupons for the district food scheme. Her volunteer had been stopped by a reporter who wanted to know whether Mara had personally administered the charitable fund. The question had already become a rumor by the time it reached the wardrobe.","Ada arrived with the public accounts and took the question outside. She pointed to the funding body's name, the payment criteria, and the place where a resident could challenge a refusal. There was no heiress required at any stage.","Mara watched from the passage, still wearing Cassian's coat. She could run across a roof to protect a document. Ada could stand on a pavement and prevent an ordinary person's dinner from disappearing inside a story. The second achievement looked less striking on camera. It mattered just as much.","June went out to record the explanation before the reporter found an easier headline.","The afternoon's plan changed. Mara would not spend it chasing Hale's courier. Rowan had the invoice reference and could trace the payment through the records available to him. June would verify the publication route. The originals would remain with the people who could establish their histories. Mara had a hearing to prepare for.","Her phone lit up with a message from counsel: the opposing side wanted the silver flower produced at the first session. Ownership would be argued before the court considered the embedded records.","Mara's fingers went to the little flower beneath her borrowed collar. For months she had fought to keep it. Tomorrow she might have to place it on a table and let somebody else decide who could carry it away.","Cassian saw the movement. He said nothing about winning. He took off his coat, held it open for her, and tucked the yellow one under his arm.","\"Leda will need this back,\" Mara said.","\"Then we had better keep the queen alive long enough to return it.\""],"questions":[{"type":"purpose","stem":"What does Leda ask Mara to do about the possible contact from her father?","options":["Arrive at the unknown location with a large group and
